\displaystyle|\widetilde{Q}-Q|,|\widetilde{m}-m|\lesssim(d-1)^{3\ell}N^{\mathfrak{o}}\Phi.

(10.11)

We start with the following estimates, which will be used later to prove
Proposition

10.3
.

Claim 10.4
.

The matrix
F
from (
10.5
) has nonzero entries only on the vertices
\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{s,s^{\prime}\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}|F_{ss^{\prime}}|\lesssim\ell(d-1)^{\ell}.\end{split}

(10.12)

Proof of
10.4
.

It is easy to see from the expression (
10.5
) that
F

has nonzero entries only on the vertices
\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
. We remark that condition on
I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})=1
, both
L
and
\widetilde{L}
are simply the Green’s function of copies of
d
-regular trees. Hence, the estimate (
3.1
) gives

\displaystyle|{\widetilde{L}}_{{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{J}}^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}|\lesssim(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\alpha^{\prime}})/2},\text{ for }{\mathsf{J}},{\mathsf{J}}^{\prime}\in\{l,a,b,c\},\quad\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in[\![{\mu}]\!].

(10.13)

By plugging (
10.13
) into (
10.5
), we get

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|F_{{{\mathsf{J}}}_{\alpha}{\mathsf{J}}^{\prime}_{\alpha^{\prime}}}|\lesssim(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\alpha^{\prime}})/2},\text{ for }{\mathsf{J}},{\mathsf{J}}^{\prime}\in\{l,a,b,c\},\quad\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in[\![{\mu}]\!],\\
&\sum_{s,s^{\prime}\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}|F_{ss^{\prime}}|\lesssim\sum_{\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}(d-1)^{-\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\alpha^{\prime}})/2}\lesssim\ell(d-1)^{\ell},\end{split}

where in the last inequality we used that
\{\alpha^{\prime}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]:\dist_{{\mathcal{T}}}(l_{\alpha},l_{\alpha^{\prime}})=2r\}=\OO((d-1)^{r})
for
0\leqslant r\leqslant\ell
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

10.3
.

For any
x,y\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
, we have

\displaystyle|(F(G^{\circ}F)^{k})_{xy}|

\displaystyle\leqslant N^{-k{\mathfrak{b}}}\sum_{s_{1},s_{2}}|F_{s_{1}s_{2}}|\sum_{s_{3},s_{4}}|F_{s_{3}s_{4}}|\cdots\sum_{s_{2k-1},s_{2k}}|F_{s_{2k-1}s_{2k}}|\leqslant N^{-k{\mathfrak{b}}}\ell^{k}(d-1)^{k\ell}

(10.14)

where in the first statement we used

|G^{\circ}_{xy}|\leqslant N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}}
uniformly for
x,y\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
from (
3.21
); in the second statement we used (
10.12
).

The claim (
10.10
) follows from (
10.9
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}|(\widetilde{G}-G)_{ww^{\prime}}|=\left|\sum_{k\geqslant 0}(GF(G^{\circ}F)^{k}G)_{ww^{\prime}}\right|&\leqslant\sum_{x,y\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}\sum_{k\geqslant 0}(\ell N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}}(d-1)^{\ell})^{k}|G_{wx}||G_{yw^{\prime}}|\\
&\leqslant\sum_{x,y\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}|G_{wx}||G_{yw^{\prime}}|\\
&\leqslant(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{x\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}(|G_{wx}|^{2}+|G_{w^{\prime}x}|^{2}).\end{split}

(10.15)

where in the first statement we used (
10.9
); in the second statement we used (
10.14
); in the third statement we sum the geometry series; in the last statement we used Cauchy-Schwartz inequality.

Next we prove (
10.11
) for
|\widetilde{Q}-Q|
, the statement for
|\widetilde{m}-m|
follows from (
5.8
).
The difference
\widetilde{Q}-Q
can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{\{u,v\}\notin\{\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}\},\{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}A_{uv}(\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}-G_{vv}^{(u)})+\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{\{u,v\}\in\{\{l_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\},\{a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha}\}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}(\widetilde{G}^{(u)}_{vv}-G_{vv}^{(u)})\\
&+\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{\{u,v\}\in\{\{l_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\},\{a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha}\}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}G_{vv}^{(u)}-\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{\{u,v\}\in\{\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}\},\{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}G^{(u)}_{vv}.\end{split}

(10.16)

For
G_{vv}^{(u)},\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}
in (
10.16
), we can rewrite them using the Schur complement formula (
2.5
),

\displaystyle G_{vv}^{(u)}=G_{vv}-\frac{G_{uv}^{2}}{G_{uu}},\quad\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}=\widetilde{G}_{vv}-\frac{\widetilde{G}_{uv}^{2}}{\widetilde{G}_{uu}}.

(10.17)

For the difference
\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}-G_{vv}^{(u)}
, using (
10.17
) and (
3.21
), we can bound it as

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\widetilde{G}_{vv}^{(u)}-G_{vv}^{(u)}|\leqslant|\widetilde{G}_{vv}-G_{vv}|+|\widetilde{G}_{uv}-G_{uv}|+|\widetilde{G}_{uu}-G_{uu}|.\end{split}

(10.18)

By plugging (
10.18
) into (
10.16
), we conclude that

\displaystyle|\widetilde{Q}-Q|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{u\sim v}(|\widetilde{G}_{vv}-G_{vv}|+|\widetilde{G}_{uv}-G_{uv}|+|\widetilde{G}_{uu}-G_{uu}|)+\frac{(d-1)^{\ell}}{N}

\displaystyle\lesssim(d-1)^{\ell}\sum_{u\sim v}\sum_{x\in\{l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha},b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}}|(G_{ux}|^{2}+|G_{vx}|^{2})+\frac{(d-1)^{\ell}}{N}\lesssim(d-1)^{3\ell}N^{\mathfrak{o}}\Phi,

where in the second statement we used (
10.10
); in the last inequality, we used the Ward identity bound.
∎

11
Proof for the self-consistent equation

In this section we prove (
4.22
) in
Theorem

4.5
.
As discussed in
Section

8
, at each iteration, we estimate (
8.1
) by performing a local resampling around
(i,o)
. We will show that the expectation breaks down into an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms in the same form.
We begin with a weighted version of (
8.1
), as presented on the left-hand side of (
11.1
) in the following proposition. Here, the additional factor
(d-1)^{3r\ell}
depends on the admissible function
R_{\bf i}
. The reader can interpret this as follows: each term in
R_{r}
(a product of
r

factors of the form (
8.16
)) is accompanied by a factor
(d-1)^{3\ell}
. Thanks to (
8.17
), even with these factors, the size of the terms remains small, i.e. bounded by
(d-1)^{3r\ell}N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}r}\ll 1
.
These factors are introduced to ensure that all the expansions in this section are
\OO(1)
-weighted sums of terms, as defined in
Definition

7.2
. Specifically, combinatorial factors are absorbed into
(d-1)^{3r\ell}
.

The proposition below expresses the expectation of Green’s functions of the graph
{\mathcal{G}}
in terms of the quantities of the new graph
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
after local resampling.

Proposition 11.1
.

Consider a forest
{\mathcal{F}}=({\bf i},E)
as in (
8.4
) and a function
(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Y)R_{\bf i}
with
R_{\bf i}\in\Adm(r,{\mathcal{G}},{\mathcal{F}})
and
r\geqslant 1
. We perform a local resampling around
(i,o)\in{\mathcal{F}}
using the resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
, denoting the new graph as
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}=T_{\bf S}({\mathcal{G}})
, with its corresponding Green’s function
\widetilde{G}
. Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{(d-1)^{3r\ell}}{Z_{\mathcal{F}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Y)R_{\bf i}\right]\\
&=\frac{(d-1)^{3r\ell}}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y)\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}\right]+\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/2}\mathbb{E}[\Psi]).\end{split}

(11.1)

Here,
\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}
is obtained by computing
R_{\bf i}
for the graph
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}
.

If we temporarily ignore the indicator and the averaging over embeddings, the
above proposition reduces to the symmetry

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\left[(G^{(i)}_{oo}-Y)R_{\bf i}\right]=\mathbb{E}\!\left[(\widetilde{G}^{(i)}_{oo}-\widetilde{Y})\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}\right]=\mathbb{E}\!\left[(\widetilde{G}^{(i)}_{oo}-Y)\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}\right]-\mathbb{E}\!\left[(\widetilde{Y}-Y)\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}\right].

(11.2)

The indicator
I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})
just restricts attention to “good” placements where we have good estimates for the Green’s function. In this regime we have
|\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}|\lesssim N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}}
and

\displaystyle|\widetilde{Y}-Y|=|Y_{\ell}(\widetilde{Q},z)-Y_{\ell}(Q,z)|\lesssim|\ell(\widetilde{Q}-Q)|\lesssim\ell(d-1)^{3\ell}N^{\mathfrak{o}}\Phi

where we used (
3.9
) and (
10.11
). So the last term in (
11.2
) is negligible. We omit the proof of
Proposition

11.1
.

The right-hand side of (
11.1
) involves the Green’s function of the switched graph
{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}
. The following two propositions help evaluate them, and express them as
\OO(1)
-weighted sums of terms involving only the Green’s function of the original graph
{\mathcal{G}}
, with negligible error. More importantly, these terms match the structure of the left-hand side of (
11.1
).

Proposition 11.2
.

Given a forest
{\mathcal{F}}=({\bf i},E)
and a function
(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Y)R_{\bf i}
with
R_{\bf i}\in\Adm(r,{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})
(recall from
Definition

8.7
). We construct
{\mathcal{F}}^{+}=({\bf i}^{+},E^{+})
(as given by (
8.5
)) by performing a local resampling around
(i,o)\in{\mathcal{F}}
with resampling data
{\bf S}=\{(l_{\alpha},a_{\alpha}),(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})\}_{\alpha\in[\![{\mu}]\!]}
, and denote
\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}=T_{\bf S}({\mathcal{G}})
.

1.

Let
r\geqslant 2
and take
R_{{\bf i}}\in\Adm(r,{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})
. Then, up to an error of size
\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/4}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
,

\displaystyle\frac{(d-1)^{3r\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y)\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}\right]

(11.3)

can be rewritten as an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms in the following form

\displaystyle\frac{(d-1)^{3r^{+}\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Y)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}],

(11.4)

where
R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\in\Adm(r^{+},{\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})
, where either
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\geqslant{\mathfrak{q}}+1
,
r^{+}\geqslant r
; or
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\geqslant{\mathfrak{q}}
,
r^{+}\geqslant r+1
.

2.

Let
R_{\bf i}=(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Q)
. Then, up to an error of size
\OO((d-1)^{-{\mathfrak{q}}\ell/2}N^{\mathfrak{o}}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}},{\widetilde{\mathcal{G}}}\in\Omega)(\widetilde{G}_{oo}^{(i)}-Y)\widetilde{R}_{\bf i}]

(11.5)

can be rewritten as an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms in the form of (
11.4
) with
R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\in\Adm(r^{+},{\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})
, where
r^{+}\geqslant 2
and
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\geqslant{\mathfrak{q}}
, or in the following form

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Y)(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)],

(11.6)

where
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\geqslant{\mathfrak{q}}+1
.

The claim (
4.22
) follows from iterating
Proposition

11.1
and
Proposition

11.2
.

Proof of (
4.22
).

We recall the sequence of forests
{\mathcal{F}}_{0}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\subset{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\subset\cdots
from
Section

8
. They encode all edges involved in local resamplings. By
7.1
and
Proposition

7.3
, up to an error
(d-1)^{2\ell}\mathbb{E}[\Psi]
,
\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(Q-Y)]
is an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms of the following two forms

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}_{1}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{G}})(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Y)(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)],

(11.7)

where
{\mathfrak{q}}\geqslant 0
; or

\displaystyle\frac{(d-1)^{3r\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}_{1}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{G}})(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Y)R_{{\bf i}_{1}}],

(11.8)

where
{\mathfrak{q}}\geqslant 0
and
r\geqslant 2
, and the function
R_{{\bf i}_{1}}\in\Adm(r,{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{G}})
.

The above expression (
11.7
) aligns with the form of
Proposition

11.1
, allowing us to apply
Proposition

11.1
and
Proposition

11.2
for the iteration process. The result in
Proposition

11.2
essentially states that, after further expansion, (
11.7
) either maintain the same form with an additional factor of
(d-1)^{-\ell/2}
, or they transform into (
11.8
).
Similarly, after expansion, (
11.8
) remains in the same form, either with an additional
(d-1)^{-\ell/2}
factor, or an extra term in the form of (
8.16
), which is bounded by
N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}}\ll(d-1)^{-\ell/2}
.
Therefore, after finitely many steps, namely
\OO(4\log_{d-1}(N)/\ell)
, all terms are bounded by
\OO(N^{-2})=\OO(\mathbb{E}[\Psi]/N)
. Meanwhile the errors from
Proposition

11.1
and
Proposition

11.2
are all bounded by
\OO((d-1)^{2\ell}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
. This gives (
4.22
).

∎

11.1
Proof of
Proposition

We denote
R_{\bf i}\in\Adm(r,{\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})
in

(as in

\displaystyle B_{0}=(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Y),\quad R_{{\bf i}}=\prod_{j=1}^{r}B_{j},

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\{G_{cc}^{(b)}-Q\}_{(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}},\quad\{G_{oc}^{(ib)},G_{ic}^{(b)},G_{ib},G_{ob}^{(i)}\}_{(i,o)\neq(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}},\\
&\{G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})},G_{bc^{\prime}}^{(b^{\prime})},G_{bb^{\prime}}\}_{(i,o)\neq(b,c),(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}},\quad(Q-m_{\rm sc}(z)).\end{split}

(11.10)

We can write (

) (with

We can replace

) with

Lemma 11.3
.

Adopt the notation and assumptions in
Proposition

11.2
, we can rewrite (
11.11
) as

\displaystyle\eqref{e:sreplace0}=\frac{(d-1)^{3r\ell}}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\prod_{j=0}^{r}\widehat{B}_{j}\right]+\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/2}\mathbb{E}[\Psi]).

(11.17)

If we temporarily ignore the indicator and the averaging over embeddings, the
total substitution error satisfies

(d-1)^{3r\ell}N^{-r{\mathfrak{b}}}\sum_{j=0}^{r}\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}_{j}|]

which follows from the bound
|B_{j}|\leqslant N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}}
in (
8.17
).
Using the same argument as in (
7.56
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}[|{\mathcal{E}}_{j}|]\lesssim(d-1)^{2\ell}\mathbb{E}[\Psi]
in all five cases above,
except for
Item

2
, where we also have the additional errors (ignoring the
indicator functions)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|Y-Q|+|\widetilde{Q}-Q|]\lesssim N^{{\mathfrak{b}}/8}\mathbb{E}[\Psi]+\mathbb{E}[(d-1)^{3\ell}N^{\mathfrak{o}}\Phi],

where we used the definitions (
4.21
) and estimate (
10.11
).
Finally, (
11.17
) follows from the fact that
r\geqslant 1
.

We can further decompose
(d-1)^{3r\ell}\prod_{j=0}^{r}\widehat{B}_{j}
in (
11.17
). The discussions in
Item

1
–
Item

5
provide the decomposition of
(d-1)^{3r\ell}\prod_{j=0}^{r}\widehat{B}_{j}
as an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms of the following form:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{(d-1)^{3\widehat{h}\ell}}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+(k_{2}+k_{3})/2)\ell}}\sum_{\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{1}}A_{\alpha_{m}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}}B_{\beta_{m}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{3}/2}C_{\gamma_{2m-1}\gamma_{2m}}R_{\widehat{h}+1-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}/2}.\end{split}

(11.18)

Here the summation for
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
runs through each
\alpha_{m},\beta_{m},\gamma_{m}
in one of the sets
[\![{\mu}]\!]
,
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
or
[\![{\mu}]\!]\setminus{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
(we recall
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
from
Figure

5
).
The factors in (
11.18
) are defined as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&A_{\alpha}=(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q),\quad B_{\beta}\in\left\{G_{c_{\beta}c}^{(b_{\beta}b)},G_{c_{\beta}b}^{(b_{\beta})}\right\}_{(b,c)\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\circ}},\quad C_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}=G_{c_{\gamma}c_{\gamma^{\prime}}}^{(b_{\gamma}b_{\gamma^{\prime}})}.\end{split}

(11.19)

Here
A_{\alpha}
originates from
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q
in (
11.13
), (
11.14
).
B_{\beta}
comes from
G_{c_{\alpha}c}^{(b_{\alpha}b)},G_{c_{\alpha}b}^{(b_{\alpha})}
in (
11.15
), (
11.16
).
C_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}
are from
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
in (
11.13
), (
11.14
). In total there are
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}/2
such terms.
R_{\widehat{h}+1-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}/2}
collects all
R_{h}
factors from
{\mathcal{U}}_{j}
as in
Item

1
–
Item

5
, with a total count of
{\widehat{h}+1-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}/2}
. Thus, the summand in (
11.18
) consists of
\widehat{h}+1
factors in total. We notice that after expansion, each
\widetilde{B}_{j}
gives at least one term, so
\widehat{h}+1\geqslant r+1
. Finally the coefficient
(d-1)^{3\widehat{h}\ell}
arises from the following crucial observation: in the replacements outlined in
Item

1
–
Item

5
, each term
(d-1)^{3\ell}B_{j}
is replaced by one of the terms
(d-1)^{3\ell}A_{\alpha},(d-1)^{3\ell}A_{\beta},(d-1)^{3\ell}C_{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}
as in (
11.19
), or a factor
R_{h}
(as in (
11.12
)) for
h\geqslant 1
with coefficient at most
(d-1)^{3h\ell}
. This leads to the following statement

Claim 11.4
.

(
11.17
) can be written as an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms of the following form:
For
\widehat{h}\geqslant r
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+(k_{2}+k_{3})/2)\ell}}\sum_{\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\frac{(d-1)^{3\widehat{h}\ell}}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\times\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right],\\
&\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}=R_{\widehat{h}+1}=\prod_{m=1}^{k_{1}}A_{\alpha_{m}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{2}}B_{\beta_{m}}\prod_{m=1}^{k_{3}/2}C_{\gamma_{2m-1}\gamma_{2m}}E(\bm{\chi}).\end{split}

(11.20)

Here
R_{\widehat{h}+1}
is the summand in (
11.18
).
In this way
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\in\Adm(\widehat{h}+1,{\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})
.
The summation for
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
is over each
\alpha_{m},\beta_{m},\gamma_{m}
in one of the sets
[\![{\mu}]\!]
,
{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
or
[\![{\mu}]\!]\setminus{\mathsf{A}}_{i}
;
and the factors in (
11.20
) are given by (
11.19
), and
E(\bm{\chi})=R_{\widehat{h}+1-k_{1}-k_{2}-k_{3}/2}
is a product of the remaining terms which depends on
\{b_{\chi},c_{\chi}\}_{\chi\in\bm{\chi}}
for some
{\bm{\chi}}\subset[\![{\mu}]\!]
.

The summation over
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
in (
11.20
) produces

(d-1)^{(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3})\ell}
terms of the form (
11.4
). Note that
this number is much larger than the normalization factor

(d-1)^{(k_{1}+(k_{2}+k_{3})/2)\ell}
in the denominator. However, as we will show
in
Lemma

11.6
, most of these terms can be seen almost immediately to be
negligible. The cancellation mechanism is similar to those in
(
7.31
) and (
7.36
). Before stating
Lemma

11.6
, we first
introduce some notation.

We view
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\chi}
as words, which are sequences of indices in
[\![{\mu}]\!]
. In particular
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})
is a word with length
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
.
Given
\bm{\chi}
, we partition words
\bm{\omega}\in[\![{\mu}]\!]^{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}}
into equivalence classes. Two words
\bm{\omega}\sim\bm{\omega}^{\prime}
are equivalent if there is a bijection on
[\![{\mu}]\!]
which preserves
\bm{\chi}
and maps
\bm{\omega}
to
\bm{\omega}^{\prime}
. We remark that the expectation in (
11.20
) depends only on the equivalence class of
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})
.

For any
f_{0},f_{1}\geqslant 0
, let
\mathsf{W}(f_{0},f_{1})
denote a set of representatives for equivalence classes of
[\![{\mu}]\!]^{k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}}
. Here, for a word
\bm{\omega}\in\mathsf{W}(f_{0},f_{1})
,
f_{0}
is the number of distinct indices (ignoring multiplicity) that do not appear in

\bm{\chi}
, and
f_{1}
is the number of these indices appearing exactly once in
\bm{\omega}
. The length of
\bm{\omega}
is
k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}
, and
f_{0}-f_{1}
of these distinct indices appear at least twice in
\bm{\omega}
. This implies

\displaystyle k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\geqslant f_{1}+2(f_{0}-f_{1})\Rightarrow k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}\geqslant 2f_{0}-f_{1}.

(11.21)

Example 11.5
.

The following are two possible terms of
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
:

\displaystyle A_{1}A_{2}B_{2}C_{47}C_{37}G^{b_{8}b}_{c_{8}c}\bigl(G^{(b_{4})}_{c_{4}c_{4}}-Q\bigr),\quad\bm{\omega}=(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})=(1,2),(2),(4,7,3,7),\quad\bm{\chi}=(8,4),

\displaystyle A_{1}A_{5}B_{5}C_{49}C_{39}G^{b_{8}b}_{c_{8}c}\bigl(G^{(b_{4})}_{c_{4}c_{4}}-Q\bigr),\quad\bm{\omega}^{\prime}=(\bm{\alpha}^{\prime},\bm{\beta}^{\prime},\bm{\gamma}^{\prime})=(1,5),(5),(4,9,3,9),\quad\bm{\chi}=(8,4).

In this example,
\bm{\omega}\sim\bm{\omega}^{\prime}
(by mapping
2
to
5
and
7
to
9
, and keeping all other indices in
[\![{\mu}]\!]
fixed). By permutation invariance of the vertices, the two terms above have the same expectation. Moreover, in
\bm{\omega}
the indices
1,2,3,7
do not appear in
\bm{\chi}=(8,4)
, and among them
1
and
3
appear only once, so
\bm{\omega}\in\mathsf{W}(4,2)
.

The expectation in (
11.20
) depends only on the equivalence class of
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})
. Moreover, for fixed
f_{0}
and
f_{1}
, the summation of
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})\sim\bm{\omega}
, contains
\OO((d-1)^{f_{0}\ell})
terms. Thus for the summation over
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})
in (
11.20
), we can first sum over the equivalence classes. We recall the summand from (
11.20
)

\displaystyle\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\frac{(d-1)^{3\widehat{h}\ell}}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\chi})\right].

(11.22)

We then have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eqref{e:oneterm}&=\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+(k_{2}+k_{3})/2)\ell}}\sum_{\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}}\eqref{e:partition2}\\
&=\frac{1}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+(k_{2}+k_{3})/2)\ell}}\sum_{f_{0},f_{1}}\sum_{\bm{\omega}\in\mathsf{W}(f_{0},f_{1})}\sum_{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})\sim\bm{\omega}}\eqref{e:partition2},\\
&=\sum_{f_{0},f_{1}}\sum_{(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})\in\mathsf{W}(f_{0},f_{1})}\frac{\OO((d-1)^{f_{0}\ell})}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+(k_{2}+k_{3})/2)\ell}}\times\eqref{e:partition2}.\end{split}

(11.23)

The following lemma states that for given
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
, the summands in the last term of (
11.23
) is either negligible, or it can be reduced to a term as in (
11.25
) below, where each index in
\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma}
appears at least twice in
R_{{\bf i}^{+}}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 11.6
.

Fix
0\leqslant f_{1}\leqslant f_{0}
satisfying (
11.21
), and a word
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})\in\mathsf{W}(f_{0},f_{1})
.
Let
\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}\subset[\![{\mu}]\!]
denote the set of indices that appear only once among

(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})
, and do not appear in
\bm{\chi}
. Then
|\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}|=f_{1}
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{(d-1)^{f_{0}\ell}}{(d-1)^{(k_{1}+(k_{2}+k_{3})/2)\ell}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\frac{(d-1)^{3\widehat{h}\ell}}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\times\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma},\bm{\chi})\right]\end{split}

(11.24)

satisfies

1.

If there exists
\alpha_{m}\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}
then
\eqref{e:oneterm1}=\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/4}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
.

2.

Otherwise,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\eqref{e:oneterm1}=\frac{(d-1)^{3(\widehat{h}+f_{1})\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(d-1)^{-f_{1}/2}R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]+\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/4}\mathbb{E}[\Psi]),\end{split}

(11.25)

where
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}=k_{1}+(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}-2f_{0}+6f_{1})\geqslant 0
, and
R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\in\Adm(\widehat{h}+f_{1}+1,{\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})
is obtained from
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
by making the following substitutions:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&G_{c_{\beta_{m}}c}^{(b_{\beta_{m}}b)}\Rightarrow G_{b_{\beta_{m}}c}^{(b)}(G_{c_{\beta_{m}}c_{\beta_{m}}}^{(b_{\beta_{m}})}-Q),\quad G_{c_{\beta_{m}}b}^{(b_{\beta_{m}})}\Rightarrow G_{b_{\beta_{m}}b}(G_{c_{\beta_{m}}c_{\beta_{m}}}^{(b_{\beta_{m}})}-Q),\quad\beta_{m}\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single},\\
&G_{c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}c_{\gamma_{2m}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m-1}}b_{\gamma_{2m}})}\Rightarrow G_{b_{\gamma_{2m-1}}c_{\gamma_{2m}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m}})}(G_{c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m-1}})}-Q),\quad\gamma_{2m-1}\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single},\quad\gamma_{2m}\not\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single},\\
&G_{c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}c_{\gamma_{2m}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m-1}}b_{\gamma_{2m}})}\Rightarrow G_{c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}b_{\gamma_{2m}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m-1}})}(G_{c_{\gamma_{2m}}c_{\gamma_{2m}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m}})}-Q),\quad\gamma_{2m-1}\notin\mathsf{I}_{\rm single},\quad\gamma_{2m}\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single},\\
&G_{c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}c_{\gamma_{2m}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m-1}}b_{\gamma_{2m}})}\Rightarrow G_{b_{\gamma_{2m-1}}b_{{\gamma_{2m}}}}(G_{c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}c_{\gamma_{2m-1}}}^{(b_{\gamma_{2m-1}})}-Q)(G_{c_{{\gamma_{2m}}}c_{{\gamma_{2m}}}}^{(b_{{\gamma_{2m}}})}-Q),\quad\gamma_{2m-1},\gamma_{2m}\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}.\end{split}

(11.26)

The first statement in
Lemma

11.6
extends (
7.30
), and the fourth claim in (
11.26
) extends (
7.32
).
Since the underlying ideas are analogous, we omit the proof.
The first three claims in (
11.26
) essentially follow from the following estimates:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\in[\![{N}]\!]}A_{c_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}A_{bc}G^{(b_{\alpha}b)}_{c_{\alpha}c}\\
&=\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\in[\![{N}]\!]}A_{c_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}A_{bc}\frac{G^{(b)}_{b_{\alpha}c}}{\sqrt{d-1}}\bigl(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b)}-Q\bigr)+\text{``negligible error,''},\\
&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\in[\![{N}]\!]}A_{c_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}A_{bc}G^{(b_{\alpha})}_{c_{\alpha}b}\\
&=\frac{1}{Nd}\sum_{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\in[\![{N}]\!]}A_{c_{\alpha}b_{\alpha}}A_{bc}\frac{G_{b_{\alpha}b}}{\sqrt{d-1}}\bigl(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q\bigr)+\text{``negligible error,''}\end{split}

(11.27)

which can be proven in the same manner as (
7.36
).
Again, we omit the proof.
We can repeat the above substitutions for all
f_{1}
indices in

\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}
, which appear only once among

(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})
and do not appear in
\bm{\chi}
.
Each substitution contributes an extra factor, and after these substitutions

\displaystyle\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\Rightarrow(d-1)^{-f_{1}/2}R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}},\quad R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}}\in\Adm(\widehat{h}+f_{1}+1,{\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}}),

(11.28)

and

{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}=2k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}-2f_{0}+6f_{1}=k_{1}/2+(k_{1}+k_{2}+k_{3}-2f_{0}+6f_{1}).

We remark that in (
11.25
), if
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\geqslant 1
, we gain an additional factor of
(d-1)^{-\ell/2}
. We do not obtain this extra factor only if
k_{1}=0,f_{1}=0
and each index in
(\bm{\alpha},\bm{\beta},\bm{\gamma})
appears exactly twice without appearing in
\bm{\chi}
(so
k_{2}+k_{3}=2f_{0}
).

Proof of the first statement in
Proposition

11.2
.

Up to a negligible error, the expression (
11.3
) can be rewritten as an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms in the form of (
11.24
). We also refer back to the more explicit expression given in (
11.18
) and (
11.20
).
If the assumptions in the first statement in
Lemma

11.6
hold, there is nothing to prove. So in the rest of the proof we can focus on the second case (
11.25
).

There are several cases in which we can apply (
11.25
), based on the decomposition of
\widehat{B}_{0}
(as in (
11.13
)) using terms
m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
,
m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}-Q)
or a term in
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}
. We treat each of these separately.

1.

Assume
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains the factor
m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
from the decomposition of
\widehat{B}_{0}
, then in (
11.25
),
k_{1}\geqslant 1
and
R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
contains a factor
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
. Let
R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}}=(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}
, then
R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\in\Adm(\widehat{h}+f_{1},{\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})
(recall from (
11.25
)). We claim that replacing
Q
with
Y
yields a negligible error. To see this, we write

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{(d-1)^{3(\widehat{h}+f_{1})\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]\\
&=\frac{(d-1)^{3(\widehat{h}+f_{1})\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Y)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right]\\
&+\frac{(d-1)^{3(\widehat{h}+f_{1})\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(Q-Y)R_{{\bf i}^{+}}\right].\end{split}

(11.29)

Note that
k_{1}\geqslant 1
, so
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\geqslant 1
, and the second term on the right-hand side of (
11.29
) is bounded as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\phantom{{}={}}\frac{(d-1)^{3(\widehat{h}+f_{1})\ell}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y||R_{{\bf i}^{+}}|\right]\\
&\lesssim\frac{(d-1)^{3(\widehat{h}+f_{1})\ell}N^{-(\widehat{h}+f_{1}){\mathfrak{b}}}}{(d-1)^{{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\ell/2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)|Q-Y|\right]\lesssim N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/2}\mathbb{E}[\Psi],\end{split}

where in the first inequality we used (
8.17
); in the second inequality we used (
4.21
) and
\widehat{h}\geqslant r\geqslant 2
.
The first term on the right-hand side of (
11.29
) is in the form of (
11.4
), by setting
r^{+}=\widehat{h}+f_{1}\geqslant\widehat{h}\geqslant r
.

2.

If (
11.20
) contains the factor
m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
from the decomposition of
\widehat{B}_{0}
, then
k_{3}\geqslant 2
in (
11.25
). There are several cases for the factor
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
contained in
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
.

If in
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
(from (
11.20
))
\alpha\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}
, then
f_{1}\geqslant 1
in (
11.25
). Also, (
11.26
) implies that
R^{\prime}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
contains
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q
. By the same argument as in (
11.29
), this leads to (
11.4
) by setting
r^{+}=\widehat{h}+f_{1}\geqslant r+1
. The same conclusion holds if
\beta\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}
.

In the remaining cases
\alpha,\beta\not\in\mathsf{I}_{\rm single}
. There are again two cases: either
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains at least two terms in the form
\{G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})},G_{cb^{\prime}}^{(b)},G_{bb^{\prime}}\}_{(b,c)\neq(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+}}
, or
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains both factors
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q),(G_{c_{\beta}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}-Q)
.

In the first case, if
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains at least two terms in the form
\{G_{cc^{\prime}}^{(bb^{\prime})},G_{cb^{\prime}}^{(b)},G_{bb^{\prime}}\}_{(b,c)\neq(b^{\prime},c^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{K}}^{+}}
, so does (
11.25
). By our assumption
r\geqslant 2
, we have
\widehat{h}+f_{1}+1\geqslant\widehat{h}+1\geqslant r+1\geqslant 3
. We remark that this is the only point in the argument where the assumption
r\geqslant 2
is required; all other parts of the proof remain valid for
r\geqslant 1
. Then (
8.18
) (with
r
taking value
\widehat{h}+f_{1}+1\geqslant 3
) implies that (
11.25
) is bounded by
\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/2}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
.

In the second case,
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains factors
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q),(G_{c_{\beta}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}-Q)
. Then they are either contained in
E(\bm{\chi})
or
k_{1}\geqslant 1
. In both cases
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}\geqslant 1
in (
11.25
). The same argument as in (
11.29
) leads to (
11.4
), by setting
r^{+}=\widehat{h}+f_{1}\geqslant\widehat{h}\geqslant r
.

3.

In the remaining case,
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains a factor
(d-1)^{3(h-1)\ell}R_{h}
from
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}
in the decomposition (
11.13
) of
\widehat{B}_{0}
. Here
h\geqslant 2
,
R_{h}
is an
S
-product term (recall from
Definition

9.1
), and it contains at least one factor of the form
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\alpha})}-Q)
or
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
. Moreover, in this case the factor
R_{h}
is included in
E(\bm{\chi})
in (
11.20
), and
\widehat{h}\geqslant r+(h-1)\geqslant r+1
.

If
R_{h}
contains at least one term of the form
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
, by the same argument as in (
11.29
), (
11.25
) leads to (
11.4
) with
r^{+}=\widehat{h}+f_{1}\geqslant\widehat{h}\geqslant r+1
.

In the other cases,
R_{h}
contains at least one term of the form
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
. By the same argument as in the second statement of
Lemma

11.6
, if
\{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}
do not appear in other terms of
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
(but
\{b_{\beta},c_{\beta}\}
do appear), we can replace
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
by
G_{b_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)/\sqrt{d-1}
); if
\{b_{\beta},c_{\beta}\}
do not appear in other terms of
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
(but
\{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha}\}
do appear), we can replace
G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
by
G_{b_{\beta}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}(G_{c_{\beta}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}-Q)/\sqrt{d-1}
); and if
\{b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha},b_{\beta},c_{\beta}\}
do not appear in other terms of
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
, we can replace it by
G_{b_{\alpha}b_{\beta}}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)(G_{c_{\beta}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\beta})}-Q)/(d-1)
. Moreover, the errors from such replacements are bounded by
\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/4}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
.
Then by the same argument as in
Item

2
, either (
11.25
) is bounded by
\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/4}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
, or (
11.25
) leads to (
11.4
) with
r^{+}\geqslant\widehat{h}+f_{1}\geqslant\widehat{h}\geqslant r+1
.

∎

Proof of the second statement in
Proposition

11.2
.

Up to a negligible error, the expression (
11.5
) can also be rewritten as an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms in the form of (
11.24
). We also refer back to the more explicit expression given in (
11.18
) and (
11.20
).
If the assumptions in the first statement in
Lemma

11.6
hold, there is nothing to prove. So in the rest of the proof we can focus on the second case (
11.25
).

We recall that in (
11.5
),
r=1
and
B_{1}=(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Q)
. If
\widehat{h}+f_{1}\geqslant 2
(from (
11.25
)), we can proceed in exactly the same manner as in the proof of (
11.3
).
Otherwise,
\widehat{h}=1
and
f_{1}=0
. We assume this scenario in the following discussion.

1.

Assume
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains
m_{\rm sc}^{2\ell+2}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
from the decomposition (
11.13
) of
\widehat{B}_{0}
.
Since
\widehat{h}=1,f_{1}=0
,
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
also contains
m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
from the decomposition of
\widehat{B}_{1}
(we recall the precise coefficients from (
7.12
)). Then (
11.25
) is of the form

\displaystyle\frac{m_{\rm sc}^{4(\ell+1)}(z)}{(d-1)^{2(\ell+1)}}\sum_{\alpha\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\frac{1}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)^{2}].

(11.30)

After replacing a copy of
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Q)
by
(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha})}-Y)
, (
11.30
) is an
\OO(1)
-weighted sum of terms in the form (
11.6
) with
{\mathfrak{q}}^{+}=2
, and the error is bounded by
\OO(N^{-{\mathfrak{b}}/2}\mathbb{E}[\Psi])
.

2.

If (
11.20
) contains
m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
from the decomposition (
11.13
) of
\widehat{B}_{0}
. Since
\widehat{h}=1,f_{1}=0
,
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
also contains
m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)(d-1)^{-(\ell+1)}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\alpha}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})}
from the decomposition of
\widehat{B}_{1}
(we recall the precise coefficients from (
7.12
)). Then (
11.25
) is of the form

\displaystyle\frac{2m_{\rm sc}^{4(\ell+1)}(z)}{(d-1)^{2(\ell+1)}}\sum_{\alpha\neq\beta\in{\mathsf{A}}_{i}}\sum_{{\bf i}^{+}}\frac{1}{Z_{{\mathcal{F}}^{+}}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)I({\mathcal{F}}^{+},{\mathcal{G}})(G_{c_{\alpha}c_{\beta}}^{(b_{\alpha}b_{\beta})})^{2}].

(11.31)

By (
8.18
) with
r=2
, the quantity in (
11.31
) is bounded by

\OO\bigl(N^{{\mathfrak{o}}}\,\mathbb{E}[\Psi]\bigr)
, where we also used that the factor

(d-1)^{-2(\ell+1)}
is canceled by the summation over
\alpha\neq\beta
,
which contains
\OO\bigl((d-1)^{2(\ell+1)}\bigr)
terms.

3.

In the remaining case,
\widehat{R}_{{\bf i}^{+}}
in (
11.20
) contains a factor
(d-1)^{3(h-1)\ell}R_{h}
with
h\geqslant 2
from
{\mathcal{U}}_{0}
in the decomposition (
11.13
) of
\widehat{B}_{0}
. Then
\widehat{h}\geqslant r+1\geqslant 2
.

∎

Proof of

(i,o)=(b_{\alpha},c_{\alpha})

\displaystyle\left(1-\left(\frac{m_{\rm sc}(z)}{\sqrt{d-1}}\right)^{2\ell+2}\right)\frac{m_{d}(z)m_{\rm sc}^{2(\ell+1)}(z)}{(d-1)Z_{{\mathcal{F}}}}\sum_{{\bf i}}\mathbb{E}\left[I({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{G}})\bm{1}({\mathcal{G}}\in\Omega)(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Y)(G_{oo}^{(i)}-Q)\right],

).
